import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { makeIo } from '../helpers/fakeio.mjs';
import { cmdCheckpoint } from '../../core/src/commands/checkpoint.mjs';
import { cmdSessionStart } from '../../core/src/commands/session-start.mjs';
import { bundlePaths } from '../../core/src/bundle/store.mjs';

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// RED — Layer-1 supervised-child no-op guard (subtask l1-supervised-guard).
//
// Source of truth: docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md
//   §"Child-hook policy (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 1; iteration 3,
//   finding 1)" and §"Acceptance constraints" constraint 1.
//
// Gate-1 findings this traces to:
//   - iteration-2 finding 1: Layer-2 main-root children collide with the
//     supervisor-owned bundle; the fix is a BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD no-op guard
//     on the hook entrypoints AND `baton checkpoint` so children never touch
//     any bundle (no write, no foreign-session rejection).
//   - iteration-3 finding 1: the guard originally MISSED `session-start`, which
//     would still read the supervisor-owned bundle and inject pending-handoff
//     resume context into the child. The fix extends the guard to EVERY
//     hook-invoked command — `session-start` included — asserting zero writes
//     AND zero resume context.
//
// CONTRACT (pinned here):
//   G1. ACTIVATION. `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD` set to ANY non-empty string
//       activates the guard. Unset OR empty string ('') is NORMAL behavior.
//   G2. SILENCE. Under the guard, every hook-invoked command exits 0 with
//       stdout === '' and stderr === '' — even with --json (no envelope).
//   G3. NO .handoff/ TOUCH. Under the guard, the command reads NOTHING under
//       `<root>/.handoff/` (bundle, journal, lock, log) and writes NOTHING
//       anywhere. "Reads NOTHING" spans content reads (readFileSync) AND the
//       metadata/discovery probes core code uses — existsSync (root discovery,
//       shared.mjs), readdirSync, statSync, lstatSync, realpathSync
//       (store.mjs / jail). The no-read property is the security contract
//       (mirrors the symlink jail test, tests/commands/gate2-iter3-group6.test
//       .mjs F12): an implementation that reads the bundle then suppresses
//       output still leaks the supervisor bundle to the child and fails here.
//       "Writes NOTHING" is asserted BOTH via a spy over every fake-fs mutator
//       (write/append/mkdir/rename/unlink/rm/copy — lock.mjs uses rm/unlink,
//       store.mjs uses rename/unlink) AND via the authoritative memfs mutation
//       history (io.fs.__history), applied to EVERY guard-active case.
//   G4. GUARD-BEFORE-PARSING (ambiguity resolved — see note below). The guard
//       check runs FIRST, before flag parsing. So a garbled invocation (a
//       stray positional) or a missing required flag STILL produces the silent
//       exit-0 no-op instead of a usage error. Pinned per command.
//   G5. THREE PLATFORMS (acceptance constraint 1). The guard-active no-op holds
//       for the hook-shaped invocations the codex and cursor adapters emit
//       (adapters/codex/hooks.json, adapters/cursor/hooks.json), not just
//       claude-code — tabled below.
//
// CONTRACT AMBIGUITY RESOLVED: the plan says the guard "quietly no-ops (exit 0,
// empty output)" but does not state whether it precedes flag parsing. Two
// readings were possible: (a) parse first, then guard (a bad invocation exits 2
// even for a supervised child); (b) guard first (a supervised child is ALWAYS
// silent). We pinned (b) — the simpler, safer contract: a supervised child must
// NEVER emit anything on stdout/stderr or set a nonzero exit, because the
// supervisor spawns these hooks and any output/exit is noise it must not have
// to filter. The guard is the very first statement of each command.
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

const T0 = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z';
const paths = bundlePaths('/repo');
const HANDOFF_PREFIX = '/repo/.handoff';
const PLATFORMS = ['claude-code', 'codex', 'cursor'];

// Representative hook-shaped `checkpoint` args per platform, mirroring the
// adapter hook manifests (adapters/<p>/hooks.json). Codex/Cursor carry an
// explicit --trigger; Cursor lower-cases its event ids.
/** @type {Record<string, string[]>} */
const CHECKPOINT_ARGS = {
  'claude-code': ['--platform', 'claude-code'],
  codex: ['--platform', 'codex', '--trigger', 'Stop'],
  cursor: ['--platform', 'cursor', '--trigger', 'stop'],
};

const snapText = (/** @type {any} */ b) => JSON.stringify(b, null, 2) + '\n';
const event = (/** @type {any} */ obj) => JSON.stringify({ schema: 'baton/event@1', ...obj });

function baseBundle(overrides = {}) {
  return {
    schema: 'baton/bundle@1',
    bundleId: 'b_guard0000000000',
    generation: 1,
    createdAt: T0,
    updatedAt: T0,
    origin: { platform: 'claude-code', model: 'claude-fable-5', sessionHint: null, unstable: false },
    task: { goal: 'g', constraints: [], acceptance: [] },
    plan: { steps: [] },
    decisions: [],
    files: { touched: [] },
    roles: { assignments: {} },
    git: null,
    handoff: { status: 'open', reason: null, reasonClass: null, toPlatformHint: null, finalizedAt: null, receive_log: [] },
    journalSeq: 0,
    compaction: { droppedDecisions: 0, note: null },
    dedupeRing: [],
    ...overrides,
  };
}

// An OPEN own-platform bundle (sessionHint null → no foreign check): the state
// where a NORMAL checkpoint appends the journal / rewrites the snapshot.
const checkpointBundle = (/** @type {string} */ platform) =>
  baseBundle({ origin: { platform, model: 'm', sessionHint: null, unstable: false } });

// A foreign, sealed bundle: the state that NORMALLY makes session-start emit a
// pending-handoff resume notice on stdout (proven at its home in
// tests/commands/session-start.test.mjs). `origin` must differ from the running
// platform for the notice to fire.
const foreignSealedBundle = (/** @type {string} */ origin) =>
  baseBundle({
    bundleId: 'b_guardss00000000',
    origin: { platform: origin, model: 'm', sessionHint: 's', unstable: false },
    handoff: { status: 'sealed', reason: 'r', reasonClass: 'usage-limit', toPlatformHint: null, finalizedAt: T0, receive_log: [] },
  });

/** A platform other than `p` (so a bundle from it reads as foreign to `p`). */
const otherPlatform = (/** @type {string} */ p) => (p === 'codex' ? 'claude-code' : 'codex');

/**
 * Instrument io.fs: record the first touch of any path under `<root>/.handoff/`
 * across BOTH content reads and metadata/discovery probes, and record every
 * mutating call the memfs fake exposes. Mirrors the read spy idiom in
 * tests/commands/gate2-iter3-group6.test.mjs (F12), extended to the full read
 * and write surfaces per test-verifier iteration-01 findings 1 and 2.
 * @param {any} io
 */
function spyFs(io) {
  const state = {
    read: /** @type {string | null} */ (null), // first .handoff/ path touched by any reader
    writes: /** @type {string[]} */ ([]), // every mutated path
  };
  const noteRead = (/** @type {any} */ p) => {
    if (state.read === null && String(p).startsWith(HANDOFF_PREFIX)) state.read = String(p);
  };

  // Readers: content + metadata/discovery. Wrap on the same object memfs
  // returned, so memfs's internal lstatSync→statSync delegation is covered too.
  for (const m of ['readFileSync', 'existsSync', 'readdirSync', 'statSync', 'lstatSync', 'realpathSync']) {
    const real = io.fs[m].bind(io.fs);
    io.fs[m] = (/** @type {any} */ p, /** @type {any} */ a) => {
      noteRead(p);
      return real(p, a);
    };
  }

  // Mutators: single-path forms + the two-path forms (rename/copy record the
  // destination, the path that gains content).
  for (const m of ['writeFileSync', 'appendFileSync', 'mkdirSync', 'unlinkSync', 'rmSync']) {
    const real = io.fs[m].bind(io.fs);
    io.fs[m] = (/** @type {any} */ p, /** @type {any} */ a) => {
      state.writes.push(String(p));
      return real(p, a);
    };
  }
  for (const m of ['renameSync', 'copyFileSync']) {
    const real = io.fs[m].bind(io.fs);
    io.fs[m] = (/** @type {any} */ from, /** @type {any} */ to) => {
      state.writes.push(String(to));
      return real(from, to);
    };
  }

  return state;
}

/** G2 silence: exit 0, no stdout (not even a --json envelope), no stderr. */
function assertSilent(io, code, label) {
  assert.equal(code, 0, `${label}: guard active exits 0`);
  assert.equal(io.stdoutText(), '', `${label}: guard active writes nothing to stdout (not even a --json envelope)`);
  assert.equal(io.stderrText(), '', `${label}: guard active writes nothing to stderr`);
}

/** G3 no-touch: zero .handoff/ reads, zero writes (spy + authoritative history). */
function assertNoTouch(io, spy, label) {
  assert.equal(spy.read, null, `${label}: guard active reads NOTHING under .handoff/ (content or metadata probe)`);
  assert.deepEqual(spy.writes, [], `${label}: guard active writes NOTHING (spy over every fake-fs mutator)`);
  assert.equal(io.fs.__history.length, 0, `${label}: the memfs mutation history is empty — the tree is byte-identical`);
}

// ===========================================================================
describe('supervised-child guard — cmdCheckpoint, tabled over all three platforms (G1–G5)', () => {
  for (const platform of PLATFORMS) {
    it(`[${platform}] guard active: a checkpoint that would normally write is a silent no-op with zero .handoff touch`, async () => {
      const io = makeIo({
        env: { BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD: '1' },
        files: { [paths.snapshot]: snapText(checkpointBundle(platform)) },
        stdin: event({ type: 'decision', payload: { summary: 'would normally append + rewrite' } }),
        now: T0,
      });
      const spy = spyFs(io);
      const code = await cmdCheckpoint(CHECKPOINT_ARGS[platform], io);
      assertSilent(io, code, `checkpoint ${platform}`);
      assertNoTouch(io, spy, `checkpoint ${platform}`);
    });

    it(`[${platform}] guard active with --json: still silent (the guard beats the --json envelope contract)`, async () => {
      const io = makeIo({
        env: { BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD: '1' },
        files: { [paths.snapshot]: snapText(checkpointBundle(platform)) },
        stdin: event({ type: 'decision', payload: { summary: 's' } }),
        now: T0,
      });
      const spy = spyFs(io);
      const code = await cmdCheckpoint([...CHECKPOINT_ARGS[platform], '--json'], io);
      assertSilent(io, code, `checkpoint ${platform} --json`);
      assertNoTouch(io, spy, `checkpoint ${platform} --json`);
    });
  }

  it('[cursor] guard active on the debounced afterFileEdit invocation: no lock, no stamp write', async () => {
    // The cursor debounce path normally acquires the repo lock (mkdir) and
    // writes a per-platform stamp under .handoff/log — the guard must skip it.
    const io = makeIo({
      env: { BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD: '1' },
      files: { [paths.snapshot]: snapText(checkpointBundle('cursor')) },
      stdin: event({ type: 'note', payload: { text: 'edit' } }),
      now: T0,
    });
    const spy = spyFs(io);
    const code = await cmdCheckpoint(['--platform', 'cursor', '--debounce', '120', '--trigger', 'afterFileEdit'], io);
    assertSilent(io, code, 'checkpoint cursor debounce');
    assertNoTouch(io, spy, 'checkpoint cursor debounce');
  });

  it('guard runs BEFORE flag parsing: a stray positional still silent no-ops (not a usage error)', async () => {
    const io = makeIo({
      env: { BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD: '1' },
      files: { [paths.snapshot]: snapText(checkpointBundle('claude-code')) },
      stdin: event({ type: 'decision', payload: { summary: 's' } }),
      now: T0,
    });
    const spy = spyFs(io);
    // Without the guard this stray token is a strict-parse usage error (exit 2).
    const code = await cmdCheckpoint(['STRAY', '--platform', 'claude-code'], io);
    assertSilent(io, code, 'checkpoint stray-token');
    assertNoTouch(io, spy, 'checkpoint stray-token');
  });

  it('guard runs BEFORE required-flag validation: missing --platform still silent no-ops', async () => {
    const io = makeIo({
      env: { BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD: '1' },
      files: { [paths.snapshot]: snapText(checkpointBundle('claude-code')) },
      stdin: event({ type: 'decision', payload: { summary: 's' } }),
      now: T0,
    });
    const spy = spyFs(io);
    // Without the guard, a missing --platform is a usage error (exit 2).
    const code = await cmdCheckpoint([], io);
    assertSilent(io, code, 'checkpoint no-platform');
    assertNoTouch(io, spy, 'checkpoint no-platform');
  });

  it('any non-empty value activates the guard (not just "1")', async () => {
    const io = makeIo({
      env: { BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD: 'yes' },
      files: { [paths.snapshot]: snapText(checkpointBundle('claude-code')) },
      stdin: event({ type: 'decision', payload: { summary: 's' } }),

     1	import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
     2	import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
     3	import { makeIo } from '../helpers/fakeio.mjs';
     4	import { cmdCheckpoint } from '../../core/src/commands/checkpoint.mjs';
     5	import { cmdSessionStart } from '../../core/src/commands/session-start.mjs';
     6	import { bundlePaths } from '../../core/src/bundle/store.mjs';
     7	
     8	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     9	// RED — Layer-1 supervised-child no-op guard (subtask l1-supervised-guard).
    10	//
    11	// Source of truth: docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md
    12	//   §"Child-hook policy (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 1; iteration 3,
    13	//   finding 1)" and §"Acceptance constraints" constraint 1.
    14	//
    15	// Gate-1 findings this traces to:
    16	//   - iteration-2 finding 1: Layer-2 main-root children collide with the
    17	//     supervisor-owned bundle; the fix is a BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD no-op guard
    18	//     on the hook entrypoints AND `baton checkpoint` so children never touch
    19	//     any bundle (no write, no foreign-session rejection).
    20	//   - iteration-3 finding 1: the guard originally MISSED `session-start`, which
    21	//     would still read the supervisor-owned bundle and inject pending-handoff
    22	//     resume context into the child. The fix extends the guard to EVERY
    23	//     hook-invoked command — `session-start` included — asserting zero writes
    24	//     AND zero resume context.
    25	//
    26	// CONTRACT (pinned here):
    27	//   G1. ACTIVATION. `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD` set to ANY non-empty string
    28	//       activates the guard. Unset OR empty string ('') is NORMAL behavior.
    29	//   G2. SILENCE. Under the guard, every hook-invoked command exits 0 with
    30	//       stdout === '' and stderr === '' — even with --json (no envelope).
    31	//   G3. NO .handoff/ TOUCH. Under the guard, the command reads NOTHING under
    32	//       `<root>/.handoff/` (bundle, journal, lock, log) and writes NOTHING
    33	//       anywhere. The no-read property is the security contract (mirrors the
    34	//       symlink jail test, tests/commands/gate2-iter3-group6.test.mjs F12):
    35	//       an implementation that reads the bundle then suppresses output still
    36	//       leaks the supervisor bundle to the child and fails here.
    37	//   G4. GUARD-BEFORE-PARSING (ambiguity resolved — see note below). The guard
    38	//       check runs FIRST, before flag parsing. So a garbled invocation (a
    39	//       stray positional) or a missing required flag STILL produces the silent
    40	//       exit-0 no-op instead of a usage error. Pinned per command.
    41	//
    42	// CONTRACT AMBIGUITY RESOLVED: the plan says the guard "quietly no-ops (exit 0,
    43	// empty output)" but does not state whether it precedes flag parsing. Two
    44	// readings were possible: (a) parse first, then guard (a bad invocation exits 2
    45	// even for a supervised child); (b) guard first (a supervised child is ALWAYS
    46	// silent). We pinned (b) — the simpler, safer contract: a supervised child must
    47	// NEVER emit anything on stdout/stderr or set a nonzero exit, because the
    48	// supervisor spawns these hooks and any output/exit is noise it must not have
    49	// to filter. The guard is the very first statement of each command.
    50	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    51

    53	const paths = bundlePaths('/repo');

/bin/zsh -lc "sed -n '1,280p' tests/adapters/claude-code-hook-script.test.mjs" in /Users/saksham/baton
 succeeded in 0ms:
import { describe, it } from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { makeIo } from '../helpers/fakeio.mjs';
import { bundlePaths } from '../../core/src/bundle/store.mjs';
// SOLE RED CAUSE for this file: the module below does not exist yet, so this
// static import throws ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND at load and every test in the file
// reds with that single, unambiguous cause. (core/* and helper imports above all
// resolve — only hook.mjs is missing.)
import { runHook } from '../../adapters/claude-code/scripts/hook.mjs';

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// ADAPTERS wave — RED. Behavioural contract for the Claude Code single hook
// entrypoint, exercised over injected io (mirrors the core command tests:
// cmdX(args, io) -> exit code). The adapter CALLS the core CLI — these tests pin
// the adapter SURFACE only (event→command mapping, the SessionStart context
// shape, fail-open), never core checkpoint/detect logic.
//
// TARGET MODULE: adapters/claude-code/scripts/hook.mjs (sole target).
//
// Source of truth: plan §Claude Code adapter hook table + platform-notes
// §Claude Code (SessionStart injects hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext).
//
// PINNED CONTRACT (open details resolved here):
//   S0. SIGNATURE. `runHook(args, io) => Promise<number>`, where args[0] is the
//       hook event name (passed by hooks.json) and io is the injected object
//       {cwd, env, stdin, stdout, stderr, fs, execFile, now, …}. The hook payload
//       arrives as the STRING io.stdin (Claude writes hook JSON on stdin).
//   S1. BATON REACH. To run a core command, hook.mjs invokes the core CLI as a
//       child process via io.execFile: it resolves the baton bin as
//       `${io.env.CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/core/bin/baton.mjs` and calls
//       io.execFile(node, [batonBin, <subcmd>, …flags], {input}). Tests assert on
//       the recorded invocation. (Verifier fold V1) EVERY core child call —
//       checkpoint AND detect — must use that resolved bin path and must forward
//       the raw hook payload as the child's stdin: opts.input === io.stdin. An
//       adapter that bypasses the core CLI or drops the payload cannot pass.
//   S2. MAPPING. Stop / PreCompact / SessionEnd -> exactly one `checkpoint
//       --platform claude-code` invocation, identifying the source event.
//       StopFailure -> BOTH a `detect --platform claude-code` invocation (the
//       classify step) AND a `checkpoint --platform claude-code` invocation
//       stamped with a stop-failure trigger; a rate_limit StopFailure carries the
//       rate_limit signal through to the checkpoint (marks the bundle limit-hit).
//   S3. SessionStart is NOT a checkpoint: it spawns NO checkpoint child. Instead
//       it reads <cwd>/.handoff/bundle.json (read-only, via io.fs) and, when a
//       PENDING (sealed OR usage-limit) bundle FROM ANOTHER PLATFORM exists, emits
//       exactly the Claude context shape on stdout:
//         {"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"SessionStart",
//                                  "additionalContext":"…suggest /baton:receive…"}}
//       (Verifier fold V2) The full pending matrix is pinned:
//         positive:  foreign SEALED bundle            -> context
//         positive:  foreign OPEN + reasonClass usage-limit (limit death before
//                    finalize — the primary failure case) -> context
//         negative:  foreign OPEN non-limit bundle    -> no context
//         negative:  own-platform open non-limit      -> no context
//         negative:  no bundle at all                 -> no context
//       proving BOTH disjuncts of "sealed OR limit-hit" AND "foreign only".
//   S4. FAIL-OPEN. Every error path (unparseable stdin, a rejected core
//       invocation, a missing/corrupt bundle) returns 0 and never throws — a hook
//       must never break the host session. Claude ignores the exit code anyway.
//
// "limit-hit" (S2/S3): pinned to the documented handoff fields — a bundle whose
// handoff.status==='sealed' OR handoff.reasonClass==='usage-limit' is "pending".
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

const T0 = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z';
const paths = bundlePaths('/repo');
const PLUGIN_ROOT = '/repo';

/**
 * Build an io whose execFile RECORDS every invocation. `respond` lets a test
 * control the child's result (resolve/reject); default resolves empty.
 */
function makeHookIo({ stdin = '', files = {}, respond } = {}) {
  const io = makeIo({ env: { CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT: PLUGIN_ROOT }, stdin, files, now: T0 });
  const calls = [];
  io.execFile = (cmd, args = [], opts = {}) => {
    calls.push({ cmd, args: Array.isArray(args) ? args : [], opts: opts || {} });
    if (respond) return respond({ cmd, args, opts });
    return Promise.resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' });
  };
  return { io, calls };
}

const line = (c) => [c.cmd, ...c.args].join(' ');
const inputOf = (c) => (typeof c.opts.input === 'string' ? c.opts.input : '');
const serialize = (c) => `${line(c)} ${inputOf(c)}`;
const findSub = (calls, sub) => calls.filter((c) => c.args.includes(sub) || new RegExp(`\\b${sub}\\b`).test(line(c)));

const BATON_BIN = `${PLUGIN_ROOT}/core/bin/baton.mjs`;
/** (V1) Every core child call goes through the core CLI with the payload forwarded. */
function assertBatonCall(c, io, label) {
  assert.ok(line(c).includes(BATON_BIN), `${label}: the invocation uses the baton bin resolved under CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT (${BATON_BIN}); got: ${line(c)}`);
  assert.equal(inputOf(c), io.stdin, `${label}: the raw hook payload is forwarded as the child's stdin (opts.input === io.stdin)`);
}

function claudeBundle(overrides = {}) {
  return {
    schema: 'baton/bundle@1',
    bundleId: 'b_hook0000000000',
    generation: 1,
    createdAt: T0,
    updatedAt: T0,
    origin: { platform: 'claude-code', model: 'claude-fable-5', sessionHint: 'sess-cc', unstable: false },
    task: { goal: 'g', constraints: [], acceptance: [] },
    plan: { steps: [] },
    decisions: [],
    files: { touched: [] },
    roles: { assignments: {} },
    git: null,
    handoff: { status: 'open', reason: null, reasonClass: null, toPlatformHint: null, finalizedAt: null, receive_log: [] },
    journalSeq: 0,
    compaction: { droppedDecisions: 0, note: null },
    dedupeRing: [],
    ...overrides,
  };
}
const snapText = (b) => JSON.stringify(b, null, 2) + '\n';
const payload = (event, extra = {}) => JSON.stringify({ hook_event_name: event, session_id: 'sess-cc', cwd: '/repo', ...extra });

// ===========================================================================
describe('hook.mjs — routine checkpoint events map to a single core checkpoint (S1, S2)', () => {
  for (const event of ['Stop', 'PreCompact', 'SessionEnd']) {
    it(`${event} -> one \`checkpoint --platform claude-code\` reaching core/bin/baton.mjs`, async () => {
      const { io, calls } = makeHookIo({ stdin: payload(event), files: { [paths.snapshot]: snapText(claudeBundle()) } });

      const code = await runHook([event], io);
      assert.equal(code, 0, 'a checkpoint hook always exits 0 (fail-open by construction)');

      const ckpts = findSub(calls, 'checkpoint');
      assert.equal(ckpts.length, 1, `${event} maps to exactly one checkpoint invocation`);
      const c = ckpts[0];
      assertBatonCall(c, io, `${event} checkpoint`);
      assert.ok(c.args.includes('--platform') && c.args.includes('claude-code'), 'checkpoint is stamped --platform claude-code');
      assert.match(serialize(c), new RegExp(event.replace('Session', 'session.?').toLowerCase(), 'i'), `the invocation identifies the ${event} source`);

      assert.equal(findSub(calls, 'detect').length, 0, `${event} is a routine checkpoint, not a detect`);
      // (V1) No core call anywhere in this event bypassed the baton bin.
      for (const any of calls) assertBatonCall(any, io, `${event} core call`);
    });
  }
});

// ===========================================================================
describe('hook.mjs — StopFailure classifies AND checkpoints (S2)', () => {
  it('a rate_limit StopFailure invokes detect and a stop-failure checkpoint carrying the rate_limit signal', async () => {
    const { io, calls } = makeHookIo({
      stdin: payload('StopFailure', { error: { type: 'rate_limit' } }),
      files: { [paths.snapshot]: snapText(claudeBundle()) },
      // Mirror real `baton detect`: usage-limit exits 10 with a JSON verdict on
      // stdout. hook.mjs must tolerate the nonzero exit (fail-open) and still route.
      respond: ({ args }) => {
        if (args.includes('detect')) {
          const e = new Error('Command failed');
          e.code = 10;
          e.stdout = JSON.stringify({ ok: true, data: { class: 'usage-limit' }, warnings: [], error: null }) + '\n';
          e.stderr = '';
          return Promise.reject(e);
        }
        return Promise.resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' });
      },
    });

    const code = await runHook(['StopFailure'], io);
    assert.equal(code, 0, 'StopFailure handling is fail-open even when detect exits nonzero');

    const detects = findSub(calls, 'detect');
    assert.equal(detects.length, 1, 'StopFailure runs the payload through `baton detect` (the classify step)');
    assertBatonCall(detects[0], io, 'StopFailure detect');
    assert.ok(detects[0].args.includes('--platform') && detects[0].args.includes('claude-code'), 'detect is stamped --platform claude-code');

    const ckpts = findSub(calls, 'checkpoint');
    assert.equal(ckpts.length, 1, 'StopFailure also writes a mechanical checkpoint');
    const c = ckpts[0];
    assertBatonCall(c, io, 'StopFailure checkpoint');
    assert.ok(c.args.includes('--platform') && c.args.includes('claude-code'), 'checkpoint is stamped --platform claude-code');
    assert.match(serialize(c), /stop-?failure/i, 'the checkpoint is stamped with a stop-failure trigger');
    assert.match(serialize(c), /rate_limit/i, 'the rate_limit signal flows through to the checkpoint (marks the bundle limit-hit)');

    // (V1) No core call anywhere in this event bypassed the baton bin.
    for (const any of calls) assertBatonCall(any, io, 'StopFailure core call');
  });
});

// ===========================================================================
describe('hook.mjs — StopFailure: detect exit codes are verdicts, not hook errors (audit finding 20)', () => {
  const LOG = '/repo/.handoff/log/hook-errors.jsonl';
  const stopFailurePayload = JSON.stringify({ hook_event_name: 'StopFailure', session_id: 's1', error: { type: 'rate_limit' } });

  it('detect exiting 10 (usage-limit verdict) writes NO diagnostics entry', async () => {
    const { io } = makeHookIo({
      stdin: stopFailurePayload,
      respond: ({ args }) =>
        args.includes('detect')
          ? Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error('Command failed'), { code: 10, stdout: '', stderr: '' }))
          : Promise.resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' }),
    });
    assert.equal(await runHook(['StopFailure'], io), 0);
    assert.equal(io.files()[LOG], undefined, 'a classification verdict is not an error — the diagnostics log stays clean');
  });

  it('detect exiting 1 (a REAL failure) still logs a diagnostics entry', async () => {
    const { io } = makeHookIo({
      stdin: stopFailurePayload,
      respond: ({ args }) =>
        args.includes('detect')
          ? Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error('Command failed'), { code: 1, stdout: '', stderr: '' }))
          : Promise.resolve({ stdout: '', stderr: '' }),
    });
    assert.equal(await runHook(['StopFailure'], io), 0);
    assert.ok(io.files()[LOG], 'a genuine failure is logged');
  });

  it('the limit-death checkpoint runs FIRST (it must land inside the 30s envelope)', async () => {
    const { io, calls } = makeHookIo({ stdin: stopFailurePayload });
    assert.equal(await runHook(['StopFailure'], io), 0);
    const order = calls.map((c) => (c.args.includes('checkpoint') ? 'checkpoint' : c.args.includes('detect') ? 'detect' : '?'));
    assert.deepEqual(order, ['checkpoint', 'detect'], 'checkpoint before detect');
    const timeouts = calls.map((c) => c.opts.timeout);
    assert.ok(timeouts.every((t) => typeof t === 'number' && t <= 18_000), `both child timeouts fit the 30s hook envelope — got ${timeouts}`);
  });
});

// ===========================================================================
describe('hook.mjs — SessionStart injects a handoff-pending notice, never checkpoints (S3)', () => {
  // CONTRACT EVOLUTION (audit finding 19, re-entered verification): the hook
  // no longer reads `${cwd}/.handoff/bundle.json` inline — that skipped root
  // discovery, so a Claude Code session launched in a repo SUBDIRECTORY never
  // saw the pending notice while its Stop checkpoints landed at the discovered
  // toplevel. It now delegates to the core `session-start` command (which owns
  // discovery, the jail, and the origin allowlist — the notice matrix is
  // pinned in tests/commands/session-start.test.mjs) and forwards the shaped
  // stdout verbatim.
  it('delegates to core session-start and forwards the shaped stdout verbatim', async () => {
    const SHAPED =
      JSON.stringify({
        hookSpecificOutput: {
          hookEventName: 'SessionStart',
          additionalContext: 'A handoff bundle from codex is pending in .handoff/ (sealed). Suggest running /baton:receive to resume that task with remapped roles.',
        },
      }) + '\n';
    const { io, calls } = makeHookIo({
      stdin: payload('SessionStart'),
      respond: ({ args }) => Promise.resolve({ stdout: args.includes('session-start') ? SHAPED : '', stderr: '' }),
    });

    const code = await runHook(['SessionStart'], io);
    assert.equal(code, 0);
    assert.equal(findSub(calls, 'checkpoint').length, 0, 'SessionStart never checkpoints');

    const ss = findSub(calls, 'session-start');
    assert.equal(ss.length, 1, 'exactly one core session-start invocation');
    assertBatonCall(ss[0], io, 'SessionStart delegation');
    assert.ok(ss[0].args.includes('--platform') && ss[0].args.includes('claude-code'), 'the delegation names the platform');

    assert.equal(io.stdoutText(), SHAPED, 'the child stdout (the hookSpecificOutput envelope) is forwarded verbatim');
  });

  it('a quiet core session-start (no pending handoff) forwards nothing', async () => {
    const { io, calls } = makeHookIo({ stdin: payload('SessionStart') });
    const code = await runHook(['SessionStart'], io);
    assert.equal(code, 0);
    assert.equal(findSub(calls, 'session-start').length, 1);
    assert.equal(io.stdoutText(), '', 'no notice, no output');
  });

  it('a failing session-start child stays fail-open (exit 0, nothing emitted)', async () => {
    const { io } = makeHookIo({
      stdin: payload('SessionStart'),
      respond: () => Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error('boom'), { code: 1 })),
    });
    const code = await runHook(['SessionStart'], io);
    assert.equal(code, 0);
    assert.equal(io.stdoutText(), '');
  });

  // The per-bundle-state notice matrix (foreign/own, sealed/open, limit/no-
  // limit, missing/corrupt) is pinned at its home — tests/commands/
  // session-start.test.mjs — since the hook only delegates (test-verifier
  // finding 3: seeded-bundle negatives here were tautological once the fake
  // child returned empty stdout). The hook-layer invariant that remains:
  it('SessionStart NEVER checkpoints, whatever the bundle state', async () => {

VERDICT: BLOCKED
FINDINGS:
1. [high] — The no-read assertions do not cover “zero reads of any path under `.handoff/`”: `spyFs` only wraps `readFileSync`, while command code can touch `.handoff` through `existsSync` during root discovery and bundle loading, and the fake fs exposes metadata reads like `readdirSync`, `statSync`, `lstatSync`, and `realpathSync`. See [supervised-child-guard.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/unit/supervised-child-guard.test.mjs:103), [shared.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/commands/shared.mjs:45), [store.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/bundle/store.mjs:126), [memfs.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/helpers/memfs.mjs:147), [memfs.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/helpers/memfs.mjs:197). — Fix: extend the read spy to wrap `existsSync`, `readdirSync`, `statSync`, `lstatSync`, and `realpathSync` for `/repo/.handoff` and descendants, or narrow the documented assertion to content reads only.
2. [high] — The no-write spy is incomplete: the test comment claims every mutating call is covered, but the wrapper only records `writeFileSync`, `appendFileSync`, `mkdirSync`, and `renameSync`; fake fs also mutates via `unlinkSync`, `rmSync`, and `copyFileSync`, and core lock/store code uses `rmSync`/`unlinkSync`. See [supervised-child-guard.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/unit/supervised-child-guard.test.mjs:94), [supervised-child-guard.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/unit/supervised-child-guard.test.mjs:109), [memfs.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/helpers/memfs.mjs:167), [memfs.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/helpers/memfs.mjs:174), [memfs.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/helpers/memfs.mjs:222), [lock.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/core/src/bundle/lock.mjs:214). — Fix: wrap all fake-fs mutators or assert `io.fs.__history.length === 0` in every guard-active command test, not only the first checkpoint case.
3. [medium] — The Claude hook guard tests prove “no spawn” but not “zero `.handoff` reads/writes”: the new hook block asserts `calls.length`, stdout, and stderr, but it does not instrument `io.fs`, so an inline-read or diagnostics-write implementation could silently pass while violating the plan. See [claude-code-hook-script.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/adapters/claude-code-hook-script.test.mjs:306), [claude-code-hook-script.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/adapters/claude-code-hook-script.test.mjs:313), [hook.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/adapters/claude-code/scripts/hook.mjs:60), [hook.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/adapters/claude-code/scripts/hook.mjs:86). — Fix: apply the same read/write/history spy to `runHook` guard-active cases and assert zero `.handoff` touches plus zero writes.
4. [medium] — The command guard tests only exercise `--platform claude-code`, while the acceptance constraint says hook commands on all three platforms must produce zero bundle writes and zero session-start resume context; Codex and Cursor templates invoke the same commands with `--platform codex` and `--platform cursor`.    294	// ===========================================================================
   295	describe('hook.mjs — BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD fast-path no-op (l1-supervised-guard)', () => {
   296	  // Source of truth: docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md
   297	  // §"Child-hook policy (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 1; iteration 3, finding 1)"
   298	  // + §"Acceptance constraints" constraint 1. When the supervisor sets
   299	  // BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD in a child's env, the hook entrypoint must fast-path
   300	  // no-op for EVERY event it handles WITHOUT spawning any baton child process:
   301	  // exit 0, empty stdout/stderr, and — critically — io.execFile is NEVER called
   302	  // (a spawned child would itself read/write the supervisor-owned bundle, which
   303	  // is exactly what the guard exists to prevent). Beyond no-spawn, the fast path
   304	  // must itself touch NOTHING under .handoff/ — no inline bundle read, no
   305	  // diagnostics write (test-verifier iteration-01 finding 3): the same io.fs
   306	  // read/write/history spy the command tests use is applied here. Unset/empty =
   307	  // normal.
   308	  const EVENTS = ['SessionStart', 'Stop', 'StopFailure', 'PreCompact', 'SessionEnd'];
   309	  const HANDOFF_PREFIX = '/repo/.handoff';
   310	
   311	  /** Record the first .handoff/ read (content + metadata) and every fs mutation. */
   312	  function spyFs(io) {
   313	    const state = { read: /** @type {string | null} */ (null), writes: /** @type {string[]} */ ([]) };
   314	    const noteRead = (p) => {
   315	      if (state.read === null && String(p).startsWith(HANDOFF_PREFIX)) state.read = String(p);
   316	    };
   317	    for (const m of ['readFileSync', 'existsSync', 'readdirSync', 'statSync', 'lstatSync', 'realpathSync']) {
   318	      const real = io.fs[m].bind(io.fs);
   319	      io.fs[m] = (p, a) => {
   320	        noteRead(p);
   321	        return real(p, a);
   322	      };
   323	    }
   324	    for (const m of ['writeFileSync', 'appendFileSync', 'mkdirSync', 'unlinkSync', 'rmSync']) {
   325	      const real = io.fs[m].bind(io.fs);
   326	      io.fs[m] = (p, a) => {
   327	        state.writes.push(String(p));
   328	        return real(p, a);
   329	      };
   330	    }
   331	    for (const m of ['renameSync', 'copyFileSync']) {
   332	      const real = io.fs[m].bind(io.fs);
   333	      io.fs[m] = (from, to) => {
   334	        state.writes.push(String(to));
   335	        return real(from, to);
   336	      };
   337	    }
   338	    return state;
   339	  }
   340	
   341	  it('every handled event fast-path no-ops without spawning ANY baton child or touching .handoff/', async () => {
   342	    for (const event of EVENTS) {
   343	      const { io, calls } = makeHookIo({ stdin: payload(event), files: { [paths.snapshot]: snapText(claudeBundle()) } });
   344	      io.env.BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD = '1';
   345	      const spy = spyFs(io);
   346	
   347	      const code = await runHook([event], io);
   348	      assert.equal(code, 0, `${event}: supervised child exits 0`);
   349	      assert.equal(calls.length, 0, `${event}: NO baton child is spawned (io.execFile never called) under the guard`);
   350	      assert.equal(io.stdoutText(), '', `${event}: no stdout under the guard`);
   351	      assert.equal(io.stderrText(), '', `${event}: no stderr under the guard`);
   352	      assert.equal(spy.read, null, `${event}: the fast path reads NOTHING under .handoff/ (no inline bundle read)`);
   353	      assert.deepEqual(spy.writes, [], `${event}: the fast path writes NOTHING (no diagnostics/log write)`);
   354	      assert.equal(io.fs.__history.length, 0, `${event}: the memfs is byte-identical after the fast path`);
   355	    }
   356	  });
   357	
   358	  it('the guard beats StopFailure\'s double-spawn (neither checkpoint nor detect runs) and touches no .handoff/', async () => {
   359	    const { io, calls } = makeHookIo({ stdin: payload('StopFailure', { error: { type: 'rate_limit' } }) });
   360	    io.env.BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD = '1';
   361	    const spy = spyFs(io);
   362	    const code = await runHook(['StopFailure'], io);
   363	    assert.equal(code, 0);
   364	    assert.equal(findSub(calls, 'checkpoint').length, 0, 'no checkpoint child under the guard');
   365	    assert.equal(findSub(calls, 'detect').length, 0, 'no detect child under the guard');
   366	    assert.equal(calls.length, 0, 'no child spawned at all');
   367	    assert.equal(spy.read, null, 'no .handoff/ read on the StopFailure fast path');
   368	    assert.equal(io.fs.__history.length, 0, 'no write on the StopFailure fast path');
   369	  });
   370	
   371	  it('any non-empty value activates the fast-path (not just "1")', async () => {
   372	    const { io, calls } = makeHookIo({ stdin: payload('Stop') });
   373	    io.env.BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD = 'yes';
   374	    assert.equal(await runHook(['Stop'], io), 0);
   375	    assert.equal(calls.length, 0, 'a non-"1" truthy value is equally a no-op');
   376	  });
   377	
   378	  it('NEGATIVE: empty-string BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD does NOT trigger the fast-path (a normal Stop still spawns a checkpoint)', async () => {
   379	    const { io, calls } = makeHookIo({ stdin: payload('Stop'), files: { [paths.snapshot]: snapText(claudeBundle()) } });
   380	    io.env.BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD = '';
   381	    assert.equal(await runHook(['Stop'], io), 0);
   382	    assert.equal(findSub(calls, 'checkpoint').length, 1, 'empty-string env is NORMAL behavior: the checkpoint child still spawns');
   383	  });
   384	});

   387	describe('hook.mjs — fail-open (S4)', () => {
   388	  it('unparseable stdin returns 0 and never throws', async () => {
   389	    const { io } = makeHookIo({ stdin: 'not json at all {', files: { [paths.snapshot]: snapText(claudeBundle()) } });

   391	    assert.equal(code, 0, 'a garbage payload soft-fails to exit 0');

   396	      stdin: payload('Stop'),
   397	      files: { [paths.snapshot]: snapText(claudeBundle()) },
   398	      respond: () => Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error('spawn failed'), { code: 1 })),
   399	    });
   400	    const code = await runHook(['Stop'], io);
   401	    assert.equal(code, 0, 'a failing child process must not break the host session');
   402	  });
   403

   405	    const { io } = makeHookIo({ stdin: payload('SessionStart'), files: { [paths.snapshot]: '{ corrupt json' } });
   406	    const code = await runHook(['SessionStart'], io);
   407	    assert.equal(code, 0, 'a corrupt bundle degrades to a quiet no-op');
   408	  });

     9	// RED — Layer-1 supervised-child no-op guard (subtask l1-supervised-guard).
    10	//
    11	// Source of truth: docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md
    12	//   §"Child-hook policy (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 1; iteration 3,
    13	//   finding 1)" and §"Acceptance constraints" constraint 1.
    14	//
    15	// Gate-1 findings this traces to:
    16	//   - iteration-2 finding 1: Layer-2 main-root children collide with the
    17	//     supervisor-owned bundle; the fix is a BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD no-op guard
    18	//     on the hook entrypoints AND `baton checkpoint` so children never touch
    19	//     any bundle (no write, no foreign-session rejection).
    20	//   - iteration-3 finding 1: the guard originally MISSED `session-start`, which
    21	//     would still read the supervisor-owned bundle and inject pending-handoff
    22	//     resume context into the child. The fix extends the guard to EVERY
    23	//     hook-invoked command — `session-start` included — asserting zero writes
    24	//     AND zero resume context.
    25	//
    26	// CONTRACT (pinned here):
    27	//   G1. ACTIVATION. `BATON_SUPERVISED_CHILD` set to ANY non-empty string
    28	//       activates the guard. Unset OR empty string ('') is NORMAL behavior.
    29	//   G2. SILENCE. Under the guard, every hook-invoked command exits 0 with
    30	//       stdout === '' and stderr === '' — even with --json (no envelope).
    31	//   G3. NO .handoff/ TOUCH. Under the guard, the command reads NOTHING under
    32	//       `<root>/.handoff/` (bundle, journal, lock, log) and writes NOTHING
    33	//       anywhere. "Reads NOTHING" spans content reads (readFileSync) AND the
    34	//       metadata/discovery probes core code uses — existsSync (root discovery,
    35	//       shared.mjs), readdirSync, statSync, lstatSync, realpathSync
    36	//       (store.mjs / jail). The no-read property is the security contract
    37	//       (mirrors the symlink jail test, tests/commands/gate2-iter3-group6.test
    38	//       .mjs F12): an implementation that reads the bundle then suppresses
    39	//       output still leaks the supervisor bundle to the child and fails here.
    40	//       "Writes NOTHING" is asserted BOTH via a spy over every fake-fs mutator
    41	//       (write/append/mkdir/rename/unlink/rm/copy — lock.mjs uses rm/unlink,
    42	//       store.mjs uses rename/unlink) AND via the authoritative memfs mutation
    43	//       history (io.fs.__history), applied to EVERY guard-active case.
    44	//   G4. GUARD-BEFORE-PARSING (ambiguity resolved — see note below). The guard
    45	//       check runs FIRST, before flag parsing. So a garbled invocation (a
    46	//       stray positional) or a missing required flag STILL produces the silent
    47	//       exit-0 no-op instead of a usage error. Pinned per command.
    48	//   G5. THREE PLATFORMS (acceptance constraint 1). The guard-active no-op holds
    49	//       for the hook-shaped invocations the codex and cursor adapters emit
    50	//       (adapters/codex/hooks.json, adapters/cursor/hooks.json), not just
    51	//       claude-code — tabled below.
    52	//
    53	// CONTRACT AMBIGUITY RESOLVED: the plan says the guard "quietly no-ops (exit 0,
    54	// empty output)" but does not state whether it precedes flag parsing. Two
    55	// readings were possible: (a) parse first, then guard (a bad invocation exits 2
    56	// even for a supervised child); (b) guard first (a supervised child is ALWAYS
    57	// silent). We pinned (b) — the simpler, safer contract: a supervised child must
    58	// NEVER emit anything on stdout/stderr or set a nonzero exit, because the
    59	// supervisor spawns these hooks and any output/exit is noise it must not have
    60	// to filter. The guard is the very first statement of each command.
    61	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    62	
    63	const T0 = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z';
    64	const paths = bundlePaths('/repo');
    65	const HANDOFF_PREFIX = '/repo/.handoff';
    66	const PLATFORMS = ['claude-code', 'codex', 'cursor'];
    67	
    68	// Representative hook-shaped `checkpoint` args per platform, mirroring the
    69	// adapter hook manifests (adapters/<p>/hooks.json). Codex/Cursor carry an
    70	// explicit --trigger; Cursor lower-cases its event ids.
    71	/** @type {Record<string, string[]>} */
    72	const CHECKPOINT_ARGS = {
    73	  'claude-code': ['--platform', 'claude-code'],
    74	  codex: ['--platform', 'codex', '--trigger', 'Stop'],
    75	  cursor: ['--platform', 'cursor', '--trigger', 'stop'],
    76	};
    77	
    78	const snapText = (/** @type {any} */ b) => JSON.stringify(b, null, 2) + '\n';
    79	const event = (/** @type {any} */ obj) => JSON.stringify({ schema: 'baton/event@1', ...obj });
    80	
    81	function baseBundle(overrides = {}) {
    82	  return {
    83	    schema: 'baton/bundle@1',
    84	    bundleId: 'b_guard0000000000',
    85	    generation: 1,
    86	    createdAt: T0,
    87	    updatedAt: T0,
    88	    origin: { platform: 'claude-code', model: 'claude-fable-5', sessionHint: null, unstable: false },
    89	    task: { goal: 'g', constraints: [], acceptance: [] },
    90	    plan: { steps: [] },
    91	    decisions: [],
    92	    files: { touched: [] },
    93	    roles: { assignments: {} },
    94	    git: null,
    95	    handoff: { status: 'open', reason: null, reasonClass: null, toPlatformHint: null, finalizedAt: null, receive_log: [] },
    96	    journalSeq: 0,
    97	    compaction: { droppedDecisions: 0, note: null },
    98	    dedupeRing: [],
    99	    ...overrides,
   100	  };
   101	}
   102	
   103	// An OPEN own-platform bundle (sessionHint null → no foreign check): the state
   104	// where a NORMAL checkpoint appends the journal / rewrites the snapshot.
   105	const checkpointBundle = (/** @type {string} */ platform) =>
   106	  baseBundle({ origin: { platform, model: 'm', sessionHint: null, unstable: false } });
   107	
   108	// A foreign, sealed bundle: the state that NORMALLY makes session-start emit a
   109	// pending-handoff resume notice on stdout (proven at its home in
   110	// tests/commands/session-start.test.mjs). `origin` must differ from the running
   111	// platform for the notice to fire.
   112	const foreignSealedBundle = (/** @type {string} */ origin) =>
   113	  baseBundle({
   114	    bundleId: 'b_guardss00000000',
   115	    origin: { platform: origin, model: 'm', sessionHint: 's', unstable: false },
   116	    handoff: { status: 'sealed', reason: 'r', reasonClass: 'usage-limit', toPlatformHint: null, finalizedAt: T0, receive_log: [] },
   117	  });
   118	
   119	/** A platform other than `p` (so a bundle from it reads as foreign to `p`). */
   120	const otherPlatform = (/** @type {string} */ p) => (p === 'codex' ? 'claude-code' : 'codex');
   121	
   122	/**
   123	 * Instrument io.fs: record the first touch of any path under `<root>/.handoff/`
   124	 * across BOTH content reads and metadata/discovery probes, and record every
   125	 * mutating call the memfs fake exposes. Mirrors the read spy idiom in
   126	 * tests/commands/gate2-iter3-group6.test.mjs (F12), extended to the full read
   127	 * and write surfaces per test-verifier iteration-01 findings 1 and 2.
   128	 * @param {any} io
   129	 */
   130	function spyFs(io) {
   131	  const state = {
   132	    read: /** @type {string | null} */ (null), // first .handoff/ path touched by any reader
   133	    writes: /** @type {string[]} */ ([]), // every mutated path
   134	  };
   135	  const noteRead = (/** @type {any} */ p) => {
   136	    if (state.read === null && String(p).startsWith(HANDOFF_PREFIX)) state.read = String(p);
   137	  };
   138	
   139	  // Readers: content + metadata/discovery. Wrap on the same object memfs
   140	  // returned, so memfs's internal lstatSync→statSync delegation is covered too.
   141	  for (const m of ['readFileSync', 'existsSync', 'readdirSync', 'statSync', 'lstatSync', 'realpathSync']) {
   142	    const real = io.fs[m].bind(io.fs);
   143	    io.fs[m] = (/** @type {any} */ p, /** @type {any} */ a) => {
   144	      noteRead(p);
   145	      return real(p, a);
   146	    };
   147	  }
   148	
   149	  // Mutators: single-path forms + the two-path forms (rename/copy record the
   150	  // destination, the path that gains content).
   151	  for (const m of ['writeFileSync', 'appendFileSync', 'mkdirSync', 'unlinkSync', 'rmSync']) {
   152	    const real = io.fs[m].bind(io.fs);
   153	    io.fs[m] = (/** @type {any} */ p, /** @type {any} */ a) => {
   154	      state.writes.push(String(p));
   155	      return real(p, a);
   156	    };
   157	  }
   158	  for (const m of ['renameSync', 'copyFileSync']) {
   159	    const real = io.fs[m].bind(io.fs);
   160	    io.fs[m] = (/** @type {any} */ from, /** @type {any} */ to) => {
   161	      state.writes.push(String(to));
   162	      return real(from, to);
   163	    };
   164	  }
   165	
   166	  return state;
   167	}
   168	
   169	/** G2 silence: exit 0, no stdout (not even a --json envelope), no stderr. */
   170	function assertSilent(io, code, label) {
   171	  assert.equal(code, 0, `${label}: guard active exits 0`);
   172	  assert.equal(io.stdoutText(), '', `${label}: guard active writes nothing to stdout (not even a --json envelope)`);
   173	  assert.equal(io.stderrText(), '', `${label}: guard active writes nothing to stderr`);
   174	}
   175	
   176	/** G3 no-touch: zero .handoff/ reads, zero writes (spy + authoritative history). */
   177	function assertNoTouch(io, spy, label) {
   178	  assert.equal(spy.read, null, `${label}: guard active reads NOTHING under .handoff/ (content or metadata probe)`);
   179	  assert.deepEqual(spy.writes, [], `${label}: guard active writes NOTHING (spy over every fake-fs mutator)`);
   180	  assert.equal(io.fs.__history.length, 0, `${label}: the memfs mutation history is empty — the tree is byte-identical`);
   181	}
   182

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	// Fake `io` object matching the injected-io contract from docs/design/core.md:
     2	// { cwd, env, stdin, stdout, stderr, fs, execFile, now }. Backed by memfs.
     3	// stdout/stderr collect writes; io.stdoutText()/io.stderrText() flatten them.
     4	// now() returns a settable ISO string (io.setNow). execFile resolves from an
     5	// execResults map keyed by the joined command string.
     6	//
     7	// Wave-B2 additions (bundle lock layer) — all optional, defaulted, and purely
     8	// additive so no prior consumer is affected:
     9	//   host / pid / startTime  — the process-identity facts the lock model writes
    10	//     into owner.json and compares on contention (see plan §Concurrency, Lock
    11	//     model iterations 3+4).
    12	//   processAlive(pid, startTime) — the "provably dead" oracle. Default: only
    13	//     THIS io's own {pid, startTime} is alive; every other descriptor reads as
    14	//     dead. Override per test to model a live foreign owner or a pid/start-time
    15	//     mismatch (reused pid).
    16	//   newFencingToken() — the injected per-acquisition token source. Deterministic
    17	//     monotonic counter ("tok-1", "tok-2", …), matching the injected-io
    18	//     discipline already used for now()/execFile so lock acquisition is
    19	//     reproducible in tests rather than drawing from Math.random.
    20	
    21	import { makeMemfs } from './memfs.mjs';
    22	
    23	export function makeIo({
    24	  files = {},
    25	  env = {},
    26	  stdin = '',
    27	  execResults = {},
    28	  now = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z',
    29	  host = 'host-A',
    30	  pid = 4242,
    31	  startTime = 111000,
    32	  processAlive = null,
    33	  fencingSeed = 1,
    34	  execPath = process.execPath,
    35	} = {}) {
    36	  const { fs, files: filesSnapshot } = makeMemfs(files);
    37	  const outChunks = [];
    38	  const errChunks = [];
    39	  let currentNow = now;
    40	  let tokenCounter = fencingSeed - 1;
    41	  const defaultAlive = (p, s) => p === pid && s === startTime;
    42	
    43	  const stdout = { write: (s) => { outChunks.push(String(s)); return true; } };
    44	  const stderr = { write: (s) => { errChunks.push(String(s)); return true; } };
    45	
    46	  const execFile = (cmd, args = [], /* opts */ _opts = {}) => {
    47	    const key = [cmd, ...(Array.isArray(args) ? args : [])].join(' ');
    48	    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    49	      if (!(key in execResults)) {
    50	        const e = new Error(`spawn ${cmd} ENOENT`);
    51	        e.code = 'ENOENT';
    52	        return reject(e);
    53	      }
    54	      const r = execResults[key];
    55	      const code = r.code ?? 0;
    56	      const out = r.stdout ?? '';
    57	      const err = r.stderr ?? '';
    58	      if (code !== 0) {
    59	        // util.promisify(execFile) rejects on nonzero exit; error carries code + streams.
    60	        const e = new Error(`Command failed: ${key}`);
    61	        e.code = code;
    62	        e.stdout = out;
    63	        e.stderr = err;
    64	        return reject(e);
    65	      }
    66	      resolve({ stdout: out, stderr: err });
    67	    });
    68	  };
    69	
    70	  return {
    71	    cwd: '/repo',
    72	    env,
    73	    stdin,
    74	    stdout,
    75	    stderr,
    76	    fs,
    77	    execFile,
    78	    now: () => currentNow,
    79	    setNow: (iso) => { currentNow = iso; },
    80	    execPath,
    81	    host,
    82	    pid,
    83	    startTime,
    84	    processAlive: processAlive || defaultAlive,
    85	    // Tiny retry budget: unit tests exercising live-lock refusal stay fast
    86	    // while the retry code path itself still runs (production default: 120x25ms).
    87	    lockRetry: { attempts: 2, delayMs: 2 },
    88	    newFencingToken: () => `tok-${++tokenCounter}`,
    89	    stdoutText: () => outChunks.join(''),
    90	    stderrText: () => errChunks.join(''),
    91	    files: filesSnapshot,
    92	  };
    93	}

     3	/**
     4	 * The --json contract: exactly one compact envelope line on stdout, nothing else.
     5	 * @param {any} io
     6	 * @param {{ok: boolean, data?: any, warnings?: any[], error?: {code: string, msg: string} | null}} env
     7	 */
     8	export function emitEnvelope(io, env) {
     9	  const full = { ok: env.ok, data: env.data ?? null, warnings: env.warnings ?? [], error: env.error ?? null };
    10	  io.stdout.write(JSON.stringify(full) + '\n');

    13	/**
    14	 * Centralized usage-error emission (gate-2 fix 11): with --json the envelope
    15	 * contract holds even for bad invocations; otherwise a one-line stderr
    16	 * message. Always returns the usage exit code 2.
    17	 * @param {any} io @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
    18	 * @param {string} cmd @param {string} msg

    21	export function usageError(io, flags, cmd, msg) {
    22	  if (flags.json === true) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: false, error: { code: 'usage', msg } });
    23	  else io.stderr.write(`baton ${cmd}: ${msg}\n`);

    27	/**
    28	 * Repo-root discovery (plan §Root discovery): `--root` wins; else the nearest
    29	 * ancestor of io.cwd carrying `.handoff/` or `baton.config.json`; else the
    30	 * nearest ancestor carrying `.git` (the repo toplevel — and a boundary the
    31	 * walk never crosses, so a nested repo resolves to itself); else io.cwd.
    32	 * @param {any} io @param {Record<string, string | boolean>} flags
    33	 * @returns {string}
    34	 */
    35	export function resolveRoot(io, flags) {
    36	  if (typeof flags.root === 'string' && flags.root.length > 0) return flags.root;
    37	  // Normalize separators up front (gate-2 iter-2 M2): a Windows cwd arrives
    38	  // backslash-separated, but Node fs accepts forward slashes, so the ascent
    39	  // and the marker checks work in one forward-slash space. The last separator
    40	  // is either kind; the loop stops at a drive/UNC or filesystem root.
    41	  const start = normSep(io.cwd);
    42	  let dir = start;
    43	  while (true) {
    44	    try {
    45	      if (io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, '.handoff')) || io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, 'baton.config.json'))) return dir;
    46	      if (io.fs.existsSync(joinNorm(dir, '.git'))) return dir; // repo toplevel; never walk past a .git boundary
    47	    } catch {
    48	      break;
    49	    }
    50	    const cut = dir.lastIndexOf('/');
    51	    if (cut < 0) break;
    52	    const parent = cut === 0 ? '/' : dir.slice(0, cut);
    53	    // At a drive-relative ceiling ("C:") examine the drive ROOT ("C:/") once —
    54	    // a repo can live directly at C:/ (iter-3 F6) — then stop.
    55	    if (/^[A-Za-z]:$/.test(parent)) {
    56	      const driveRoot = `${parent}/`;
    57	      if (driveRoot !== dir) {
    58	        dir = driveRoot;
    59	        continue;
    60	      }
    61	      break;
    62	    }
    63	    if (parent === dir) break;
    64	    dir = parent;
    65	  }
    66	  return start;
    67	}

    69	/** The supported platform enum, validated wherever a platform id is intake. */
    70	export const PLATFORMS = ['claude-code', 'codex', 'cursor'];
    71	
    72	/**
    73	 * Validate a platform id against the enum (surface-audit fold): a typo'd
    74	 * platform used to silently seed persistent state whose bogus origin then
    75	 * rejected the REAL platform's checkpoints as foreign.
    76	 * @param {string | boolean | undefined} value
    77	 * @returns {string | null} an error message, or null when valid/absent
    78	 */
    79	export function platformError(value) {
    80	  if (value === undefined) return null;
    81	  if (typeof value === 'string' && PLATFORMS.includes(value)) return null;
    82	  return `platform must be one of ${PLATFORMS.join('|')} (got ${JSON.stringify(value)})`;
    83	}

    85	/**
    86	 * Strict flag parser (surface-audit fold; docs/design/core.md §tooling always
    87	 * specified strict parsing). spec maps each flag name to 'string' | 'boolean';
    88	 * `json` and `root` are implied common flags. Errors — unknown flag, stray
    89	 * positional, missing string-flag value — return {error} so the command exits
    90	 * 2 instead of silently losing intent: the lenient parser let a stray token
    91	 * flip SAFETY flags off (checkpoint/doctor --strict, remap --native-only,
    92	 * receive --print-prompt all read fail-dangerous exit 0).
    93	 * @param {string[]} args
    94	 * @param {Record<string, 'string' | 'boolean'>} spec
    95	 * @returns {{flags: Record<string, string | boolean>, error?: string}}
    96	 */
    97	export function parseFlagsStrict(args, spec) {
    98	  /** @type {Record<string, 'string' | 'boolean'>} */
    99	  const full = { json: 'boolean', root: 'string', ...spec };
   100	  /** @type {Record<string, string | boolean>} */
   101	  const flags = {};
   102	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
   103	    const a = args[i];
   104	    if (!a.startsWith('--')) return { flags, error: `unexpected argument '${a}' — flags are --name [value]` };
   105	    const key = a.slice(2);
   106	    const kind = full[key];
   107	    if (kind === undefined) return { flags, error: `unknown flag --${key}` };
   108	    if (kind === 'boolean') {
   109	      // A following token that is not a flag would have been this flag's
   110	      // silent "value" under the lenient parser; reject it loudly.
   111	      const next = args[i + 1];
   112	      if (next !== undefined && !next.startsWith('--')) {
   113	        return { flags, error: `unexpected token '${next}' after --${key} (a bare flag takes no value)` };
   114	      }
   115	      flags[key] = true;
   116	      continue;
   117	    }
   118	    const next = args[i + 1];
   119	    // A string flag consumes the next token unless it is itself a KNOWN flag
   120	    // (so `--commit --origin x` errors instead of eating '--origin').
   121	    if (next === undefined || (next.startsWith('--') && full[next.slice(2)] !== undefined)) {
   122	      return { flags, error: `--${key} requires a value` };
   123	    }
   124	    flags[key] = next;
   125	    i += 1;
   126	  }
   127	  return { flags };
   128	}
   129	
   130	/**
   131	 * Minimal flag parser: `--key value` pairs (value = next token not starting
   132	 * with --), bare `--key` booleans, positionals collected in order.
   133	 * @param {string[]} args
   134	 * @returns {{flags: Record<string, string | boolean>, positionals: string[]}}
   135	 */
   136	export function parseFlags(args) {
   137	  /** @type {Record<string, string | boolean>} */
   138	  const flags = {};
   139	  /** @type {string[]} */
   140	  const positionals = [];
   141	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
   142	    const a = args[i];
   143	    if (a.startsWith('--')) {
   144	      const key = a.slice(2);
   145	      const next = args[i + 1];
   146	      if (next !== undefined && !next.startsWith('--')) {
   147	        flags[key] = next;
   148	        i += 1;
   149	      } else {
   150	        flags[key] = true;
   151	      }
   152	    } else {
   153	      positionals.push(a);
   154	    }
   155	  }
   156	  return { flags, positionals };
   157	}

     1	// In-memory fs fake for unit tests. Synchronous subset only.
     2	// Atomicity contract: renameSync moves content in a single step, so a target
     3	// path is never observed carrying partial content. Every mutation appends a
     4	// full snapshot to `fs.__history`, letting tests enumerate intermediate states
     5	// and prove the tmp+rename discipline (target's first appearance is a rename).
     6	
     7	function fsError(code, syscall, path) {
     8	  const e = new Error(`${code}: ${syscall} '${path}'`);
     9	  e.code = code;
    10	  e.syscall = syscall;
    11	  e.path = path;
    12	  return e;
    13	}
    14	
    15	function normalize(p) {
    16	  const str = String(p);
    17	  const isAbs = str.startsWith('/');
    18	  const out = [];
    19	  for (const part of str.split('/')) {
    20	    if (part === '' || part === '.') continue;
    21	    if (part === '..') {
    22	      out.pop();
    23	      continue;
    24	    }
    25	    out.push(part);
    26	  }
    27	  return (isAbs ? '/' : '') + out.join('/');
    28	}
    29	
    30	function dirname(np) {
    31	  const i = np.lastIndexOf('/');
    32	  if (i <= 0) return '/';
    33	  return np.slice(0, i);
    34	}
    35	
    36	function basename(np, parent) {
    37	  return parent === '/' ? np.slice(1) : np.slice(parent.length + 1);
    38	}

    40	export function makeMemfs(initialFiles = {}) {
    41	  const store = new Map(); // path -> string content
    42	  const dirs = new Set(['/']);
    43	  const mtimes = new Map();
    44	  const history = [];
    45	  let clock = 0;
    46	
    47	  const snapshot = () => Object.fromEntries(store);
    48	  const record = (op, paths) => history.push({ op, paths, files: snapshot() });
    49	  const touch = (np) => mtimes.set(np, ++clock);
    50	
    51	  const mkdirp = (np) => {
    52	    let cur = '';
    53	    for (const part of np.split('/').filter(Boolean)) {
    54	      cur = cur + '/' + part;
    55	      dirs.add(cur);
    56	    }
    57	  };
    58	
    59	  const ensureParent = (np, syscall) => {
    60	    const parent = dirname(np);
    61	    if (parent === '/' ) return;
    62	    if (!dirs.has(parent)) throw fsError('ENOENT', syscall, np);
    63	  };
    64	
    65	  for (const [p, content] of Object.entries(initialFiles)) {
    66	    const np = normalize(p);
    67	    mkdirp(dirname(np));
    68	    store.set(np, String(content));
    69	    touch(np);
    70	  }
    71	
    72	  const fs = {
    73	    readFileSync(p /*, enc */) {
    74	      const np = normalize(p);
    75	      if (!store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'open', np);
    76	      return store.get(np);
    77	    },
    78	
    79	    writeFileSync(p, data /*, enc */) {
    80	      const np = normalize(p);
    81	      ensureParent(np, 'open');
    82	      store.set(np, String(data));
    83	      touch(np);
    84	      record('writeFileSync', [np]);
    85	    },
    86	
    87	    appendFileSync(p, data) {
    88	      const np = normalize(p);
    89	      ensureParent(np, 'open');
    90	      store.set(np, (store.get(np) || '') + String(data));
    91	      touch(np);
    92	      record('appendFileSync', [np]);
    93	    },
    94	
    95	    renameSync(from, to) {
    96	      const nf = normalize(from);
    97	      const nt = normalize(to);
    98	      if (store.has(nf)) {
    99	        ensureParent(nt, 'rename');
   100	        const content = store.get(nf);
   101	        store.delete(nf);
   102	        store.set(nt, content);
   103	        touch(nt);
   104	        record('renameSync', [nf, nt]);
   105	        return;
   106	      }
   107	      // Directory rename (atomic move of the node and all descendants) — the
   108	      // lock's dead-reclaim arbitration renames the stale lock dir aside.
   109	      if (dirs.has(nf)) {
   110	        ensureParent(nt, 'rename');
   111	        const pre = nf + '/';
   112	        dirs.delete(nf);
   113	        dirs.add(nt);
   114	        for (const k of [...store.keys()]) {
   115	          if (k.startsWith(pre)) {
   116	            store.set(nt + k.slice(nf.length), store.get(k));
   117	            store.delete(k);
   118	          }
   119	        }
   120	        for (const d of [...dirs]) {
   121	          if (d !== nt && d.startsWith(pre)) {
   122	            dirs.add(nt + d.slice(nf.length));
   123	            dirs.delete(d);
   124	          }
   125	        }
   126	        touch(nt);
   127	        record('renameSync', [nf, nt]);
   128	        return;
   129	      }
   130	      throw fsError('ENOENT', 'rename', nf);
   131	    },
   132	
   133	    mkdirSync(p, opts = {}) {
   134	      const np = normalize(p);
   135	      if (opts.recursive) {
   136	        mkdirp(np);
   137	        record('mkdirSync', [np]);
   138	        return undefined;
   139	      }
   140	      ensureParent(np, 'mkdir');
   141	      if (dirs.has(np) || store.has(np)) throw fsError('EEXIST', 'mkdir', np);
   142	      dirs.add(np);
   143	      record('mkdirSync', [np]);
   144	      return undefined;
   145	    },
   146	
   147	    existsSync(p) {
   148	      const np = normalize(p);
   149	      return store.has(np) || dirs.has(np);
   150	    },
   151	
   152	    readdirSync(p) {
   153	      const np = normalize(p);
   154	      if (!dirs.has(np)) {
   155	        if (store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOTDIR', 'scandir', np);
   156	        throw fsError('ENOENT', 'scandir', np);
   157	      }
   158	      const names = new Set();
   159	      const collect = (k) => {
   160	        if (k !== np && dirname(k) === np) names.add(basename(k, np));
   161	      };
   162	      for (const k of store.keys()) collect(k);
   163	      for (const d of dirs) collect(d);
   164	      return [...names];
   165	    },
   166	
   167	    unlinkSync(p) {
   168	      const np = normalize(p);
   169	      if (!store.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'unlink', np);
   170	      store.delete(np);
   171	      record('unlinkSync', [np]);
   172	    },
   173	
   174	    rmSync(p, opts = {}) {
   175	      const np = normalize(p);
   176	      const isFile = store.has(np);
   177	      const isDir = dirs.has(np);
   178	      if (!isFile && !isDir) {
   179	        if (opts.force) return;
   180	        throw fsError('ENOENT', 'rm', np);
   181	      }
   182	      if (isFile) store.delete(np);
   183	      if (isDir) {
   184	        const pre = np + '/';
   185	        if (!opts.recursive) {
   186	          const hasChildren = [...store.keys(), ...dirs].some((k) => k !== np && k.startsWith(pre));
   187	          if (hasChildren) throw fsError('ENOTEMPTY', 'rmdir', np);
   188	          dirs.delete(np);
   189	        } else {
   190	          for (const k of [...store.keys()]) if (k === np || k.startsWith(pre)) store.delete(k);
   191	          for (const d of [...dirs]) if (d === np || d.startsWith(pre)) dirs.delete(d);
   192	        }
   193	      }
   194	      record('rmSync', [np]);
   195	    },
   196	
   197	    statSync(p) {
   198	      const np = normalize(p);
   199	      if (store.has(np)) {
   200	        const size = Buffer.byteLength(store.get(np));
   201	        return { isFile: () => true, isDirectory: () => false, isSymbolicLink: () => false, size, mtimeMs: mtimes.get(np) || 0 };
   202	      }
   203	      if (dirs.has(np)) {
   204	        return { isFile: () => false, isDirectory: () => true, isSymbolicLink: () => false, size: 0, mtimeMs: mtimes.get(np) || 0 };
   205	      }
   206	      throw fsError('ENOENT', 'stat', np);
   207	    },
   208	
   209	    // The fake cannot represent symlinks, so lstat === stat and realpath is
   210	    // normalization. Symlink-refusal behavior is proven over the real fs in
   211	    // tests/integration/symlink-escape.test.mjs.
   212	    lstatSync(p) {
   213	      return fs.statSync(p);
   214	    },
   215	
   216	    realpathSync(p) {
   217	      const np = normalize(p);
   218	      if (!store.has(np) && !dirs.has(np)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'realpath', np);
   219	      return np;
   220	    },
   221	
   222	    copyFileSync(src, dest) {
   223	      const ns = normalize(src);
   224	      const nd = normalize(dest);
   225	      if (!store.has(ns)) throw fsError('ENOENT', 'copyfile', ns);
   226	      ensureParent(nd, 'copyfile');
   227	      store.set(nd, store.get(ns));
   228	      touch(nd);
   229	      record('copyFileSync', [ns, nd]);
   230	    },
   231	  };
   232	
   233	  // Introspection hook for atomicity assertions; not part of the real fs API.
   234	  fs.__history = history;
   235	
   236	  return { fs, files: () => snapshot() };
   237	}

     8	const RESUME_HINT = {
     9	  'claude-code': 'Suggest running /baton:receive to resume that task with remapped roles.',
    10	  codex: "Run 'baton receive --platform codex' or the baton-handoff skill to resume that task with remapped roles.",

    14	/**
    15	 * `baton session-start` — the cheap SessionStart gate the adapters call
    16	 * instead of full receive preparation (gate-2 fix 8). Inspects only
    17	 * pending-foreign state: a bundle that is sealed OR limit-hit AND from
    18	 * another platform yields a harness-shaped notice on stdout; every other state
    19	 * — own bundle, open non-limit bundle, no bundle, corrupt tree — is a quiet
    20	 * no-op. Soft paths ALWAYS exit 0 (a session-start notice must never break a
    21	 * session); a mis-parsed invocation is a broken hook manifest, which is loud
    22	 * (exit 2) so init/doctor drift gets noticed. With --json (surface-audit fold)
    23	 * the standard envelope wraps the outcome so machine consumers can tell "no
    24	 * pending handoff" from a swallowed failure: data = {pending, shaped}.
    25	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    26	 * @returns {Promise<number>}
    27	 */
    28	export async function cmdSessionStart(args, io) {
    29	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(args, { platform: 'string' });
    30	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'session-start', parsed.error);
    31	  const flags = parsed.flags;
    32	  const finish = (/** @type {boolean} */ pending, /** @type {any} */ shaped) => {
    33	    if (flags.json === true) emitEnvelope(io, { ok: true, data: { pending, shaped } });
    34	    else if (shaped !== null) io.stdout.write(typeof shaped === 'string' ? `${shaped}\n` : JSON.stringify(shaped) + '\n');
    35	    return 0;
    36	  };
    37	  try {
    38	    const platform = typeof flags.platform === 'string' ? flags.platform : null;
    39	    if (!platform || platformError(platform) !== null) {
    40	      io.stderr.write('baton session-start: --platform <claude-code|codex|cursor> is required — emitting nothing\n');
    41	      return finish(false, null);
    42	    }
    43	
    44	    const root = resolveRoot(io, flags);
    45	    // Full load (journal replay included): an unsealed limit death may live
    46	    // only as a post-snapshot journal note. Load warnings are deliberately
    47	    // not surfaced — this is a notice path, not a diagnostics path. An UNSAFE
    48	    // managed tree (symlinked .handoff — threat model / F12) is a quiet no-op:
    49	    // never emit context derived from a tree that failed the jail.
    50	    const { bundle, unsafe } = loadBundle(root, io);
    51	    if (bundle === null || unsafe === true) return finish(false, null);
    52	
    53	    const origin = bundle?.origin?.platform;
    54	    const pending = bundle?.handoff?.status === 'sealed' || bundle?.handoff?.reasonClass === 'usage-limit';
    55	    const foreign = typeof origin === 'string' && origin !== platform;
    56	    if (!pending || !foreign) return finish(false, null);
    57	
    58	    const kind = bundle.handoff.status === 'sealed' ? 'sealed' : 'limit-hit before sealing';
    59	    // The bundle is untrusted input (threat model, iter-3 F12): allowlist the
    60	    // origin label — never interpolate a crafted origin string into injected
    61	    // session context.
    62	    const label = typeof origin === 'string' && platformError(origin) === null ? origin : 'another platform';
    63	    const msg = `A handoff bundle from ${label} is pending in .handoff/ (${kind}). ${RESUME_HINT[platform] ?? RESUME_HINT.codex}`;
    64	
    65	    if (platform === 'cursor') return finish(true, { additional_context: msg });
    66	    if (platform === 'claude-code') return finish(true, { hookSpecificOutput: { hookEventName: 'SessionStart', additionalContext: msg } });
    67	    return finish(true, msg);
    68	  } catch {
    69	    return finish(false, null);
    70	  }
    71	}

    24	/**
    25	 * Opt-in transcript-tail capture (gate-2 fix 10). Returns the bounded,
    26	 * redacted tail or null when the gate is closed: config off (the default),
    27	 * not a PreCompact payload, no usable transcript_path, or an unreadable /
